### 26 Oct 2026 Workflow 22 follow-up: recent caller context

The subsequent user call still was not flawless. Its transcript begins with a 3,929 ms first-token reply, followed by replies at 886 ms, 1,762 ms, 1,009 ms, 864 ms, 1,060 ms and 1,189 ms; the date turns then drift between Hindi and English, repeat the service/date/time intake, and produce a truncated/uncertain date phrase. Production traces for run 418 independently show Qwen first-output timings of 2,063 ms, 1,157 ms and 4,163 ms; the last response took 6,725 ms total (1,101 prompt tokens, 751 cached, 53 completion tokens). This remains far above the requested 1.0–1.5 s target.

Root cause found in caller state: only validated name and phone were persisted for prompts. Short utterance history can be trimmed, losing the room request and date fragments. Added a bounded call-scoped memory of up to five recent caller statements (500 characters total) and include it as untrusted caller data in the local Qwen reminder. Assistant utterances are excluded; the instruction explicitly says date fragments are not confirmations. Deployed to the production API after backing up both server source files to `/root/kodewaves-rollback/recent-caller-context-20261026/`. Runtime smoke validated retention of a room request, “tomorrow,” and a date fragment without incorporating an assistant question; API restarted and health returned 200.

This improves context retention but is not yet accepted as a conversation fix: no fresh post-deploy caller test has verified date follow-up, spoken language consistency, phone transcription, or continuous TTS. First-token delay, date/time extraction and confirmation, language detection quality, TTS pacing, and telephony remain open.

### 26 Oct 2026 Workflow 22 live call 417 and reply-guard deployment

The user-provided call transcript and production traces do **not** show flawless behavior. Run 417's cold Qwen first text took 3,929 ms with a 5,144 ms total on a 976-token prompt (751 cached); later first-text times were about 0.86–1.76 s. The call preserved a partial booking date poorly: after the caller supplied a date fragment and the assistant had already proposed a possible October date, a later turn fell back to the generic service/date/time intake question. Date/time were present as runtime context; guessing a date or claiming confirmation would be unsafe, but repeating the entire intake is still incorrect. STT confidently labeled one utterance Santali (`sat`, 0.978), which is not in the installed TTS inventory; the response route could therefore diverge from the transcript language. TTS request/transport traces showed maximum inter-PCM gaps of 311, 539, 1,266, 1,146 and 2,152 ms across sampled utterances, while the output trace showed up to 1,320 ms internal silence; a transport-only trace did not show equivalent long delivery gaps. Audio continuity remains unaccepted.

After protected active-call telemetry returned zero, applied a narrow source-only patch to the three production bind-mounted API files: (1) unsupported detected languages now retain raw STT metadata but route LLM/TTS language hints to an actually installed local voice (Hindi initially, or the previous supported language); (2) the local Qwen guard now explicitly carries booking details forward, asks only the next missing item, clarifies ambiguous dates without restarting intake, and forbids inventing year/time; (3) no deterministic booking/date confirmation was added. Originals are retained at `/root/kodewaves-rollback/reply-language-guard-20261026/`. Restarted only `kodewaves_api`; public `/api/v1/health` returned 200, Docker health is healthy, restart count is zero, and protected active-call count returned zero. In-container runtime smoke verified Santali routes to Hindi and the booking guard loads. Local focused suite: 74 tests passed; the API suite was run without repo `conftest.py`, with a no-op `loudness` import stub for Pipecat's unused audio utility, so this is targeted unit evidence, not full-suite acceptance. No code was committed or pushed, and no tests or reports were added to GitHub.

This is a production prompt/routing guard, not proof of better answers, dates, speed, TTS continuity, or caller-heard audio. Next acceptance requires a fresh Workflow 22 call covering a date fragment, explicit date clarification, supported Hindi/English switch, and one unsupported STT-language utterance. Record actual playback, no repeated intake, and same-turn LLM/STT/TTS timing; the 700–800 ms target remains unmet.

### 29 October 2026 Qwen prompt-cache probe and phone-number STT candidate

Using the already-running production Ollama model through the API container (one synthetic request at a time, no file/model/container changes), a compact Hindi streaming probe returned its first text in 1.015 s and completed in 1.914 s. A more workflow-like 509-token request with eight short dialogue messages and one `assist_caller` tool returned first output in 4.613 s and completed in 5.656 s. Repeating that identical request reused 505/509 prompt tokens from Ollama's prefix cache: first output was 0.148 s and total completion 1.311 s. This directly shows the cost of a cold/changed prompt prefix on this CPU model and that the current warm cache can remove most prefill delay. Earlier production evidence from Workflow 22 run 408 recorded 1,314 prompt tokens, only 512 cached, a 5,438-character system prompt and two tools before an 11.450 s first output. That aligns with prompt prefill/changed-prefix size as a major bottleneck, though the synthetic probe does not establish why run 449 reached 10.348/15.339 s or provide p50/p95. No model/runtime setting was changed.

The shared branch adds a phone-slot-only STT candidate path: while collecting a phone number, Hindi remains the primary IndicConformer decode, and an English candidate is attempted only when its decoder is already resident. It replaces the transcript only if it recovers more explicit digits, preserves the Hindi reply-language route, and leaves the existing digit-by-digit phone readback/confirmation in place. A partial Hindi result triggers rescue; if Hindi already returns 10–15 digits, the candidate is skipped because an equal-length English result would not replace it. The focused local run passed **29** tests, including mocked API wiring, candidate selection/skip logic and existing spoken-number parser/readback regressions; syntax and whitespace checks pass. The extra test coverage includes the full available voice-contact regression file.

For a read-only A/B using two digit-heavy segments from the saved run 449 audio, one Hindi decode produced 5 explicit digit groups in 222 ms while the English candidate produced 8 in 177 ms, so the rescue rule would select English. Another segment produced 7 in Hindi and 0 in English, so Hindi remains selected; the unnecessary English request is avoided whenever Hindi already has a complete 10–15 digit number. Full caller intent/phone-slot alignment and caller-heard result are not established by these isolated segments. This remains **uncommitted and undeployed** pending a safe merge into the production checkout, which currently has extensive uncommitted changes in the same STT/API files. No audio text or phone number was printed.

### 29 October 2026 repeated-date hallucination fix

The live booking helper accepted only a transcript consisting exactly of “tomorrow” or “today”. Workflow 22's noisy STT example, “Tomorrow's telepm”, therefore fell through to Qwen, which supplied an unsupported 10:00 AM. A second path also returned no deterministic response when the caller repeated the same date after an unclear time. Updated the production helper to recognize supported relative-date words inside noisy transcripts and to keep asking for the missing time when the date repeats. It does not select a time or create a booking.

Applied the narrow change to the server working source and the live API container after the protected fleet count was zero; preserved the pre-change source in `/tmp` and tagged rollback image `kodewaves-api:rollback-booking-time-20261029`. Committed the running API snapshot as `kodewaves-api:booking-time-repeat-20261029` and updated the existing Compose image tag `kodewaves-api:candidate-contact-020776c1` to that snapshot, preserving the previous image under `kodewaves-api:rollback-candidate-contact-020776c1`. Restarted only `kodewaves_api`; it returned healthy with HTTP 200, zero active calls, and in-container smoke checks returned “Okay, 30 October 2026. What time works for you?” for both first and repeated “Tomorrow's telepm.”

Local verification: the voice-contact regression, contact-context and booking-tool-choice suites passed **86/86**. This validates the deterministic helper, not a fresh caller-heard WebRTC or telephone call. The phone parser already reads a valid recognized number one digit at a time and waits for confirmation; the delegated review found no parser defect. Spoken-number accuracy is still an STT concern: retained production logs contain no recent correlated STT/LLM traces, so actual primary/rescue recognition quality and long-turn latency remain unverified. The 700–800 ms target and full platform acceptance remain open.

### 29 October 2026 WebRTC pre-connect cancellation and phone-digit scoring

Production logs for WebRTC runs 450 and 451 showed ICE candidate pairs remaining `IN_PROGRESS`, with no connected event. While that initial negotiation was still pending, the output worker rejected pre-connect audio frames every 500 ms and cancelled the call after 10 failures (~5 s), earlier than the peer-connection timeout. This is a transport lifecycle defect distinct from LLM/TTS latency. Updated SmallWebRTC to discard only pre-connect mixer frames without counting them as write failures; post-connect loss and a connection closed before ICE still follow the normal failure path. The same server STT fix adds regional-script digit forms to the phone-rescue scorer so correct Bengali, Tamil, Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam, Punjabi and Marathi digit transcripts are not scored as zero.

Verification: local SmallWebRTC transport tests **13/13 passed**; Indic STT focused regression tests **3/3 passed**; `git diff --check` passed. Deployed the transport change in the running API snapshot (`kodewaves-api:phone-flow-overlay-20261029`) and retained `kodewaves-api:rollback-phone-flow-before-webrtc-preconnect-20261029`. Deployed the STT scorer change and retained `kodewaves-indic-stt:rollback-phone-flow-before-regional-20261029`. Both API and STT containers returned healthy; an in-container check confirmed pre-connect audio is dropped while post-connect failure remains reported, and regional digit counts return expected values. No fresh client WebRTC call was run after deployment. Thus the early five-second cancellation path is corrected, but successful ICE connectivity, caller-heard speech, telephone behavior, measured end-to-end latency, language quality and full platform acceptance remain unverified.

### 29 October 2026 Coturn public relay and port-range correction

Further inspection of the same failed WebRTC sessions showed the candidate `172.19.0.8` was the live Coturn container address. The AA Panel Compose service had no `external-ip` mapping, so a relay allocation exposed a Docker-private address. It also published UDP ports 49152–49350 while leaving Coturn on its default relay range; a live TURN allocation returned port 50308, outside the published range. Updated the AA Panel Compose service to map `SERVER_IP` as the public relay address and constrain allocations to 49152–49350. The API image and STT image were not changed in this step.

Production Compose validation passed. Coturn was recreated alone; API remained healthy. A live TURN REST-authenticated allocation now returns public relay addresses on `72.61.123.8` and ports inside the published range (observed 49174, 49268 and 49269). This proves TURN allocation and the advertised relay mapping from inside the server network, but it does not prove an external browser can reach the relay or that a complete WebRTC call succeeds. Fresh caller-heard WebRTC/telephone tests, latency percentiles and full platform acceptance remain open.

### 29 October 2026 mixed-language phone-number STT scoring correction

The phone parser accepts Hindi phonetic ASR forms of English digit words, including `नीन`, `थी`, and `आइट`, but the STT phone-rescue scorer did not count these as digits. During phone capture this could make a more complete Hindi decode appear weaker than an incomplete resident English decode, causing the rescue candidate to replace it. Added the missing spellings to the scorer and regression cases for the user's 10-digit spoken-number pattern. The number still requires parser validation/readback; this fix does not guess unclear digits.

Local focused Indic STT/contact tests passed (27 tests). The AA Panel Compose STT image was rebuilt and `kodewaves_indic_stt` is running healthy as `kodewaves-indic-stt:candidate-da240807`; its runtime source contains the new aliases. A post-deploy live call has not yet confirmed the caller-heard digits, and this does not fix overall LLM latency or prove every supported language's recognition quality.

This confirms cold/load or prefix state can dominate and that warm first-token latency is fast, but full reply generation still exceeds one second and one sample is not p50/p95. The 2B model is again resident `Forever` at 1.6 GiB and the temporary candidate has been removed. No model change is justified; reduce/canonicalize changing prompt prefixes and verify on real calls before further selection changes.

## Current production roadmap — 28 October 2026

This is the single execution roadmap. Historical entries below are evidence to locate and recheck, not proof that today's release passes. The user wants completion today; the release decision remains conditional on actual acceptance results. The whole platform is not currently ready. Do not assign an overall percentage from file edits, commands, or isolated tests.

### Ownership and deployment

| Owner | Work and file boundary | Production authority |
|---|---|---|
| Root agent | LLM latency, runtime clock, integration, roadmap, release checks | Reviews every diff and deploys changes sequentially after checking both workers and fleet for active calls. |
| `contact_flow` subagent | Caller memory, phone fragments/digits and date retention; `contact_context.py`, `spoken_digits.py` | Candidate changes and isolated checks only; no rollout. |
| `model_selectors` subagent | Model, STT language and TTS voice selection; `AIModelConfigurationV2Editor.tsx`, `sttEngineType.ts` | Candidate changes only; no rollout or build. |
| `audio_streaming` subagent | Phrase ordering, completion, interruption; `speaches_tts.py`, `local_speech_aggregator.py` | Candidate changes and pure checks only; no model inference or rollout. |

Use the existing `codex/production-integration` branch. Production source is authoritative; a local file is compared with production before copying. Preserve dirty work and unrelated sites. Model inference and benchmarks run on the VPS, with no unrelated active call during measurement. Keep source/image rollback copies, change only affected services, verify health after rollout, and retain an exact before/after deployment record. Tests, temporary scripts and this tracker stay out of GitHub.

### Confirmed findings and immediate work

29 October STT/LID routing fix: the local Indic STT API returns both `language` (decoder used, potentially a fallback or phone-rescue decoder) and `detected_language` (language identified by LID). The adapter had been using the primary decoder value as the detected language, so transcript/reply/TTS language could remain Hindi when LID identified another supported language. The adapter now retains decoder language separately, uses detected language plus confidence/stability policy for auto routing, preserves phone-rescue metadata, and normalizes locale-form English confidence; pinned-language behavior remains unchanged. Hindi-decoder/Marathi-LID, `en-IN` confidence, and phone-rescue separation regressions pass: **18/18** focused tests locally. Deployed API-only on 29 Oct as `kodewaves-api:tts-phrase-48-20261029`, image `sha256:76b75e1215bb64e2d7751ddd001d3669d145b99bbed3876f1d55551601a2f643`; source SHA256 `d5dd7d6422f49a804b800d5a368bf9bd516f9724e7bbb62515eedeb5708ba75a`. Public `/api/v1/health` returned `ok`; API is healthy with zero restarts. Prior live image is preserved as `kodewaves-api:rollback-stt-lid-20261029` (`sha256:0b4e5cc2ced27b18d02ba5604b8f0752e15bfb4e8d3e0edea85d8dd4d2e6ecb9`); the pre-existing candidate image is separately preserved. STT, TTS, LLM, UI, data and network containers were not rebuilt or restarted. A caller-heard language-switch test is still needed; this deploy proves the adapter is live, not that every detected language has an installed/qualified voice. Existing generic routing tests passing earlier did not cover the split decoder/LID result, so the older statement below that rejected missing metadata wiring should not be read as proof this narrower routing defect was absent.

29 October scoped audits (read-only): prompt-template CRUD and its organization-scoped API are present in the dirty integration tree, but template-to-agent acceptance and org-isolation tests are missing. The live STT container `/health` and `/v1/models` confirm `status=ok`, `supports_auto_detect=true`, and 15 installed/detectable codes: `as,bn,brx,doi,en,hi,kn,mai,ml,mr,ne,pa,sa,ta,te` (14 Indian-language codes plus English). Auto is an installed runtime capability; the original “Auto unavailable” screenshot is stale relative to this live manifest. Runtime readiness is confirmed, not caller-heard quality or code-switch continuity. The admin Models page does not clearly expose this regional inventory. For live phone-number failures, available examples point first to mixed/corrupted upstream STT transcripts; parsing intentionally asks again rather than guessing. For TTS gaps, serial phrase synthesis through a single-worker gateway is a plausible cause, not a confirmed caller-side root cause. Use `voice_output_trace`/first-speech-frame and gap metrics rather than first-PCM timing alone. These audit findings are not completion evidence.

29 October language qualification audit: current runtime is 14/22 scheduled Indian STT/TTS languages plus Indian English. The STT code's `AUTO_DETECT_LANGUAGES` intentionally filters out Gujarati, Kashmiri, Konkani, Manipuri, Odia, Santali, Sindhi, and Urdu even though the detector class list contains those codes; current manifest cannot be expanded by changing the installed-language environment alone. TTS has the same overall 15-code manifest and partial gender coverage above. Candidate review found no ready CPU replacement that is verified against all 22, Indian-accented English, code-switching, voice consistency, low latency, and commercial telephony: Indic-Parler claims 20 scheduled languages plus English but omits Kashmiri and Punjabi and lacks CPU streaming/latency evidence; Indic-Speak claims 22+English but is a gated 3B model with restrictive terms that conflict with automated calling; IndicF5 covers only 11 languages and no English; MMS-TTS is non-commercial; Indic-Mio lacks a valid CPU audio/latency result in this environment. No candidate was downloaded or installed from this audit. Model capability claims require per-language audio/accuracy, CPU resource and p50/p95 first-audible measurements before activation.

29 October admin call kill-switch candidate: the admin endpoint now sends a correlated worker-sync termination request to the process owning the WebRTC/telephony pipeline, waits for the runner/transport shutdown, and only then marks the run complete and reports billing. Failed termination attempts are retryable; immediate abort skips final LLM extraction. The focused suite passes **9/9**, and the existing immediate-abort extraction regression passes **1/1** in this checkout. No production deployment or carrier/browser call has verified this path; keep F26 open until a controlled live acceptance and post-deploy health check.

29 October local selector round-trip regression: a new API-level test calls the model-configuration v2 save and reload route handlers with an auto-capable installed STT manifest, then confirms `language="multi"` survives stored configuration, saved/reloaded response, and effective STT config. A stale phrase test was corrected to compare `Aggregation.text`; this only repairs the test assertion and does not prove caller-audible continuity. Production traces still lack recent per-phrase/playback gap events, so no live TTS gap root cause or fix is claimed.

29 October local campaign sync race fix: source imports could unconditionally overwrite a concurrent operator pause/cancel with `running`/`completed`, then publish an event that could dispatch contacts. Campaign transitions now use an atomic `state == syncing` condition; non-empty imports stop before publishing if the operator changed the state, and failure handling preserves a pause/cancel. The focused race regressions pass **2/2** locally. No production deployment or live campaign acceptance has occurred.

The API/DB path now marks the appointment `cancelled`, preserving the record while freeing it from active availability. The focused regression passed **1/1** in this checkout. No production deployment or live booking acceptance has occurred.

28 October shared audio-tail correction: traced a stop/play failure to `pipecat/src/pipecat/transports/base_output.py`. On a natural output-queue timeout, the shared media sender calls `_bot_stopped_speaking`, which had cleared any partial PCM buffered below one transport chunk. A production-source reproduction showed 60 ms of audio had 40 ms queued and 20 ms retained; the timeout discarded that 20 ms before the next phrase. Changed the natural stopped-speaking notification to preserve that tail. Explicit interruptions still clear their buffer, and `handle_tts_stopped` still flushes trailing audio at true completion. Preserved the old server source at `/root/kodewaves-rollbacks/audio-tail-20261028/base_output.py.before`; deployed source SHA256 `aefdf71dc2fc7e46d64effdaa6fdbb429c57e7037c67c072874d1406cd68076b`, rollback SHA256 `2de2a73855dea903637b2de59a3c942fd30abb45defdc34236764d82b9befd52`. Restarted only API after both workers showed zero active calls; API returned healthy. A smoke against the loaded production Pipecat module passed preservation on natural stop and clearing on explicit interruption. This code is shared by browser and telephone outputs; it does not resolve synthesis underflow itself or prove caller-heard continuity without a fresh call.

28 October deployed speech inventory and auto-selector update: the live IndicConformer service manifest reports `supports_auto_detect=true` for `as-IN`, `bn-IN`, `brx-IN`, `doi-IN`, `en-IN`, `hi-IN`, `kn-IN`, `mai-IN`, `ml-IN`, `mr-IN`, `ne-IN`, `pa-IN`, `sa-IN`, `ta-IN`, and `te-IN`. The live VITS/Rasa manifest reports the same 15 language codes and 22 voices; Indus Pocket TTS is bilingual Hindi/Indian English, while regional scripts route to language-specific VITS voices. Some languages have only one gender-labelled voice, and Indian-English gender/voice uniformity is not established. Rebuilt the dirty production checkout's UI as `kodewaves-ui:stt-selector-20261028` (`sha256:79b8b78d5dc3df2c98016b12586a4e7bf78f39e04fba392a2893acb07dcf3dea`), smoke-started it in isolation (`/api/health` returned `ok` and its static bundle contains the multilingual selector), tagged the prior `kodewaves-ui:v2.0.0` as `kodewaves-ui:rollback-before-stt-selector-20261028`, then replaced only the UI container. The new UI and both API workers returned healthy/HTTP 200; the other speech/model containers stayed running. No browser-level save/reload test could be done because local browser access is disabled in Work settings. Inventory advertises capability, not proven transcription/TTS quality for every language or complete end-to-end behavior.

With both API workers reporting zero active calls, saved the old source at `/root/kodewaves-rollbacks/phone-fragments-20261028/contact_context.py`, deployed candidate SHA `70ab8266e81052fc583110e3bd5544a2bb3f3e1a5ddb418901e5c1eadc63e151`, and restarted only the API container. Post-restart deployed-source smoke passed all three reproduced cases, both API ports returned HTTP 200, and the API container is healthy. Seven mocked endpoint cases, eight legacy/default endpoint cases, twelve capability cases and two persistent capability checks passed on the VPS. 28 October live-source workflow prompt probe: the production Workflow 22 configuration and registered tool list were loaded on the VPS, without executing tools or writing customer data. The adapter hid the booking tool before confirmation, so the two synthetic requests actually sent zero tool schemas. First request: first text 2,520 ms, 1,018 prompt tokens / 779 cached. Follow-up: 873 ms, 1,073 / 1,014 cached; it incorrectly answered in Hindi and reversed who should give the details despite the caller's English question. These are two direct model requests, not a complete call or a quality pass. 28 October typed English A/B on the VPS: with the real composed Workflow 22 prompt and identical synthetic two-turn history, baseline returned Hindi and confused who should supply the name at 3,079 ms first text; a staged conservative English hint returned English requesting name, phone, service and future date/time at 3,386 ms. Both cached 779 tokens; candidate used 38 additional input tokens. The change applies only without a speech detector and preserves explicit language preference. Six isolated language-routing checks passed. This single example does not establish live voice behavior, broad quality or a latency improvement. Candidate remains staged, not deployed.

28 October trace audit: the realtime observer's `tts_first_audio` records the first nonempty PCM frame, which may contain silence. When the optional call-event recorder is absent, `voice_output_trace` separately reports the first output frame above its RMS speech threshold, relative to final STT; it is a server-side estimate, not actual browser or carrier playback. When the recorder is enabled, the observer suppresses that separate output trace, and the recorder records first nonempty TTS audio rather than first audible playback. Thus neither metric proves speech-end-to-ear latency. Any acceptance report must identify the precise stage and call path; a genuine browser/carrier playback timestamp is still required.

28 October language-routing regression: a caller's explicit English/Hindi reply preference was applied to LLM guidance but not shared with TTS. TTS could therefore follow the previous STT language and synthesize the new-language response with the old voice route. A candidate now publishes a per-call reply-language override from `CallerLanguageContext`, clears it when automatic recognition moves to a different source language, and makes local TTS honor it. Three candidate assertions passed in the production API image (explicit English override, automatic switch clears stale override, and Hindi/English detector routing). The integrated checkout ran the focused suite: 9 tests passed in 5.82 s with isolated placeholder DB/Redis URLs and no database connection. This change is committed only on the isolated client integration branch; production has not been restarted, and actual caller-heard voice switching remains unverified.

Added the missing explicit English/Hindi preference handoff from `CallerLanguageContext` to TTS, reduced the initial PCM cushion from 400 ms to 200 ms, forced closure of an active audio context when the final HTTP phrase fails after earlier PCM, and treated spoken “plus” as a phone-number separator. The API image was built as a small layer over the existing production image, avoiding dependency/model rebuilds. Image `sha256:4f9a7e3fe01aa6f938243334554aa964f5c9dab6fa932b33fa7219a49064c3c9` is now `kodewaves-api:v2.0.0`; prior image `sha256:e5e448122947c30b0df5a8eb64be950cbea78fa6c1a3db4cff0d546af7d0a30a` is preserved as `kodewaves-api:rollback-voicefix-20261028`. 28 October production follow-up: production `caller_phone_fragment_reply` had a second edge case: “and” and similar spoken separators were allowed through token validation but remained in the `unknown` list, so a complete otherwise-valid number could not be stored. Removed only those separators after the phone slot is active. A minimal API layer `sha256:2d5cc9fe96649ab3c64acedec1ec37db4e2e07a3d917d1612cfb7895e920d375` is live as `kodewaves-api:v2.0.0`; the immediately previous API image is preserved as `kodewaves-api:rollback-phone-separator-20261028`, and the source backup is `api/services/workflow/contact_context.py.pre_codex_phone_separator_20261028`. Image-isolated smoke passed on “and” grouped digits, “plus nine one” country code, and ambiguous “on” still requiring a retry. API health is `ok`, the other stack containers remain running, zero API error/traceback lines were seen in the next five minutes, and 4.7 GiB RAM / 98 GiB disk were available. Thus number parsing has a code-level fix, but there is no new caller-heard confirmation; STT quality, conversational memory/booking flow, TTS continuity, and actual latency remain open.

28 October LLM stream warmup check: the warmup sidecar had only exercised native `/api/generate`, while voice uses OpenAI-compatible streamed `/v1/chat/completions`. With the same resident Qwen 3.5 2B model and short 33-token synthetic prompt, first visible content was 1,573 ms on the first compatible stream and 126 ms on its repeat. Native `/api/chat` with thinking disabled returned first content in 174 ms. The direct native non-stream sample took 746 ms total for 12 output tokens (132 ms prompt evaluation; 607 ms generation). Updated the existing mounted warmup sidecar to exercise the compatible streaming route once at startup and after a model load; it does not perform recurring inference while the model is already resident. The helper was manually exercised before restarting the sidecar, so the later 215 ms request is not a clean measurement of the sidecar's cold-to-warm effect. The sidecar restarted and logged `stream warmed`; the API and model containers were not restarted. These are synthetic, short-prompt endpoint measurements, not a production workflow turn; they identify a first-use stream-path penalty but do not explain or resolve the 15–20 s workflow outliers. Actual STT-to-audible latency still needs a post-change call.

28 October prompt-cache diagnostic: on the VPS resident model, the same synthetic 1,331-token OpenAI-compatible request returned first visible text in 13,556 ms on its first run. An exact repeat returned first text in 173.6 ms and reported 1,327 cached prompt tokens (1,331 total). This is strong evidence that cold prompt-prefix processing can dominate a first uncached request in this direct model probe. It is not a real call, does not identify which sub-operation consumed the 13.4 seconds, and does not establish that every Workflow 22 request will hit the prefix cache. The production log currently has no Workflow 22 trace in the last 30 minutes, so no live call could be correlated. No model/configuration was changed based on this synthetic result. Next acceptance evidence is a fresh production `llm_stream_trace` with prompt/cache counts plus first-audible timing; caller-heard latency remains unverified.

28 October phone-correction guard: an unclear attempted replacement could leave an earlier saved `caller_phone`, `phone_just_completed`, or `approved_phone` available to a later booking. The parser now clears those values only when a number attempt was actually made and could not be safely parsed; unrelated changed-intent speech keeps the saved number. Isolated smoke on the newly deployed API passed for unclear replacement clearing, “and” separators, and preserving the value on an unrelated cancellation request. Image `sha256:cdb819fbfbef20bc4a610cf05ca6b7218a750f396e78a673a6d02a020ab4e9cb` is live as `kodewaves-api:v2.0.0`; previous image `sha256:2d5cc9fe96649ab3c64acedec1ec37db4e2e07a3d917d1612cfb7895e920d375` is preserved as `kodewaves-api:rollback-phone-stale-20261028`, with source backup `api/services/workflow/contact_context.py.pre_codex_stale_phone_20261028`. API and health endpoint are healthy, active call count was zero at restart and remains zero, and no API errors appeared in the five-minute check. No model, STT, TTS, UI, or database was changed. This state fix does not correct ASR digit recognition or prove caller-heard booking behavior; no post-deploy Workflow 22 call has yet been recorded.

The clock placement diagnostic used a real composed workflow prompt with custom tools omitted; it is not an equivalent production-call benchmark. In its follow-up comparison, cached prompt tokens increased from 779 to 998 and first text changed from 2.743 s to 2.293 s. Its first baseline request also had a 26.514 s outlier. That outlier and the actual two-tool workflow remain acceptance work. Do not report the 450 ms difference as the full flow being fixed.

### Shared speech and conversation flow

```text
Browser microphone / telephone media
  -> audio normalization and endpointing
  -> selected or supported automatic STT route
  -> raw transcript + recognition language/confidence
  -> validated caller state and explicit reply-language preference
  -> workflow node, permitted tools and stable prompt
  -> exact safe reply OR streamed model text / actual tool result
  -> natural clause aggregation without splitting words, numbers or dates
  -> installed language/voice route -> first non-silent PCM
  -> ordered transport playback -> transcript and run persistence
```

Every turn needs a shared run/turn identity and timestamps for speech end, VAD stop, STT request/final, LLM request/first useful text, first speakable clause, TTS request/first PCM/first non-silent PCM, actual playback, cancellation and completion. A deterministic reply's 0 ms model time must not be presented as a measured model response or end-to-end latency.

Language behavior must follow installed capabilities rather than cap the product to Hindi/English. Current installed paired inventory is `as, bn, brx, doi, en, hi, kn, mai, ml, mr, ne, pa, sa, ta, te`; each remains subject to individual accuracy, voice and timing qualification. Keep the eight user-deprioritized languages inactive. Hinglish is a mixed Hindi/English path, not an extra independently verified language. Preserve explicit recognition mode. In automatic mode, select a supported decoder, answer in the caller's requested/detected supported language and keep the chosen voice persona stable. Never claim that installed inventory proves quality.

### Execution order and release gates

1. **Trace and reproduce the failing voice path.** Freeze current image/source identifiers and select a synthetic corpus covering enquiries, support, corrections, number/date dictation, known contacts, booking and unavailable actions. Distinguish current failures from pre-fix traces.
2. **Close the LLM and recognition defects.** Review the clock patch's actual follow-up effect; isolate remaining cold stalls and CPU contention. Apply only demonstrated changes. Run equivalent quality cases before any model/runtime replacement. Correct language and phone/date handling through shared state, not a booking-only script.
3. **Close speech continuity.** Verify the first and last words, internal gaps, language switching, interruption and stale-frame cancellation on the browser path. Check both short and long replies and match transcript completion to audible completion.
4. **Verify telephone parity.** One controlled authorized call after browser acceptance, then the same relevant corpus through the carrier path. Separate unavoidable carrier setup from platform delay. Confirm run, recording and post-call state.
5. **Finish user/admin journeys below.** Reproduce each issue, fix its owner layer, deploy affected artifacts, and test persistence and failure handling. A rendered page alone is not acceptance.
6. **Release audit.** Recheck security findings against current production, reconcile code/build/deployment identity, verify rollback and repeat only gates touched by the final changes. Publish an evidence ledger with remaining failures. Keep the release incomplete when a required gate fails.

The preferred speech target remains 700–800 ms; the user's practical interim target is 1.0–1.5 s. Measure from actual speech end to first audible response and report p50/p95, cold/warm, concurrency 1/2 and browser/telephone separately. Suggested stage budgets are endpointing <=200 ms, STT <=250 ms, first useful model text <=350 ms, first non-silent synthesis <=250 ms and transport/playback <=250 ms. These are targets, not current measurements. Acceptance corpus: 30 warm and 5 cold turns across Hindi, English and Hinglish, plus individual regional-language cases; exercise both short and long replies. No inferred latency from model size, total synthesis speed, HTTP bytes, or cached greeting alone.

### Full-platform completion ledger

All journeys below require fresh acceptance; historical unit/static checks are supporting evidence only. For each journey record the deployed revision/image, action/run identifier, expected and observed result, saved backend state, failure case and tenant/role check.

| Area / user and admin pages | Required end-to-end behavior and negative cases | State |

| Organization model configuration, admin settings/models | Provider/model/voice/language selection resolves faithfully at runtime; installed/available/quality status is honest; cloud/BYOK errors and key isolation work. | Partial code/live evidence; full gate open |
| Text chat, WebRTC, live transcript and run detail | Keep earlier messages and caller memory, recover send/version errors, preserve completed speech, expose accurate timing/error states and reconnect safely. | Open |
| Telephony configurations and routing | Persist inbound/outbound configuration, verify signed webhooks and organization routing, handle provider failures, hang up actual calls and preserve other tenants. | Open |
| Campaign list/new/edit/detail, automation | Contact import mappings and digits preserved; preflight, queue/admission, launch/pause/retry/stop, opt-out, outcomes and billing agree. | Open |
| CRM | Import/create/edit/search/timeline, reuse existing caller details with consent, clear saved values, correct relationships and tenant isolation. | Open |
| Appointments | Available service/date/time rules, future IST validation, conflict handling, explicit confirmation, persisted result ID, edit/cancel and no false success. | Open |
| Tools and integrations | Tool scope follows node, arguments/results validated, secrets protected, failure/retry/idempotency checked; no unsupported action claims. | Open |
| Files, recordings and run artifacts | Upload/download/delete with ownership, playback and retention; transcript/audio refer to the correct run; interrupted/failed runs finish consistently. | Open |
| Forms | Enforce active state and schema server-side, validate submission, isolate tenants and show saved result/errors. | Open |
| Widgets and embeds | Published widget routes to correct tenant/agent, public/session authorization, config changes and conversation lifecycle work. | Open |
| Prompt templates | Correct public/private visibility and mutation permissions; creating/editing an agent uses the selected saved template. | Open |
| Plans, pricing, credit packages, billing/wallet/payments | Enforced plan features/limits, verified receipts/webhooks, idempotent grants/debits and usage reconciliation; use configured sandbox for monetary tests. | Open |

No final production-readiness claim until the required voice, action, security, billing and user/admin journeys have matching live evidence. If the CPU host cannot meet the latency gate with a quality-qualified stack, report that measured limit and a concrete capacity/runtime alternative rather than fabricate readiness.

## Status rules

- **Verified live**: deployed and exercised through the real user journey, with saved result and failure handling checked.
- **Code verified**: implementation or automated test passed, but the live journey remains untested.
- **Open**: a known failure, missing implementation, or missing acceptance evidence.
- Each completed row must link to the commit/file, test result, deployment and run/action evidence. Do not turn an old audit finding into a current defect without rechecking it.

The API logged 502 fallback-proxy errors with `ui:3010` DNS failure. Joined the running UI to the API network, then added `kodewaves-shared` to the UI service in the default Compose file so future deployments retain the connection. Server checkout is at `35ae46d3`; `docker compose config --quiet` passed and API-to-UI health returned HTTP 200. No speech model was changed. A fresh Workflow 22 call trace is still pending; no `voice_stage_trace`, `voice_tts_trace`, or `voice_output_trace` event appeared during the observation window.
- 25 October 2026 isolated Indic-Mio Q8 GGUF trial: pinned 624 MiB file was loaded into a separate Ollama container limited to 0.5 CPU and 2 GiB RAM; production Ollama was untouched and protected active-calls was 0 before the run. Two Hindi one-sentence requests each generated 82–88 model tokens and returned an empty chat content field. One run: wall 14.03 s, load 4.09 s, prompt eval 1.0 s for 44 tokens, generation 8.91 s for 88 tokens. Container memory was 933 MiB while running. This is a capped throughput diagnostic, **not** an end-to-end speech benchmark or evidence that the candidate meets the voice/latency target: the codec was not run and the Ollama output was unusable. Trial container and its anonymous model volume were removed; pinned source/GGUF remain outside the project under `/root/kodewaves-candidates/indic-mio-gguf` for a correctly wired codec trial if justified.
- 25 October 2026 isolated Qwen2.5 1.5B trial: official Ollama 986 MiB model loaded only into a separate 0.5-CPU, 2-GiB container after protected active-calls reported zero. With a short receptionist prompt, the Hindi turn took 10.2 s to first text and 16.7 s total, and incorrectly asked for the time already supplied. The English follow-up switched language in 1.4 s to first text but lost the booking/name context and greeted again. CPU cap, prompt differences and cold load prevent a production speed comparison; the two behavior failures are sufficient to reject this particular replacement as-is. Trial container/volume removed; production Qwen3.5 2B remained loaded.
- 25 October 2026 model settings reconciliation: organization 1 currently saves local Qwen `qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M`, local IndicConformer `indicconformer-ctc-int8` with recognition language `hi`, and combined local TTS `indic-vits-rasa-13` with preferred voice `ENG_IN`. Running sidecars advertise one combined TTS with 15 language codes/22 voice IDs and one STT with 23 language codes but no automatic-language capability. `ENG_IN` and `HIN_F` both route to the same Indus/Inaya bilingual synthesis service; they do not represent two different installed Hindi/English engines. Local STT Auto is hidden because the installed model cannot identify spoken language; enabling that control without a verified LID path would misstate capability. DhVaani and Parler endpoints were unreachable and are not advertised as installed. Master catalog currently contains many enabled but UNTESTED cloud rows; the available catalog filters by active provider credentials. These facts do not establish full 22-language speech or naturalness.

- Faithful adapter recheck: previous native-endpoint model probes omitted some production sampling settings and are not exact runtime benchmarks. Repeated the two synthetic turns using `create_llm_service_from_provider`, service `system_instruction`, `LLMContext`, and `get_chat_completions` on the VPS. Current Qwen2B Q4 first text took 10.071 and 5.403 seconds; answers repeated a greeting or asked a generic question. A compact synthetic role prompt reduced first text to 3.271 and 2.064 seconds but still produced generic/inconsistent replies. This supports prompt-prefill cost as a contributor while leaving language, grounding and name continuity unaccepted. No saved prompt/model selection was changed. The actual engine sets its system instruction in service settings rather than duplicating it in conversation history; no duplicate-system root cause was found in that path. These probes omit parts of a full live call such as resolved persona, transport and audio and cannot prove end-to-end latency.
- Server model comparison: current Qwen3.5 2B Q4 produced first visible text at 9.346 seconds for a composed receptionist help prompt and 5.593 seconds for a supplied-name English follow-up; replies were generic/unrelated. A Qwen3.5 0.8B candidate from the official Ollama registry was downloaded only on the VPS and tested with the saved agent prompt: first Hindi reply 13.953 seconds including cold startup, next English follow-up 4.496 seconds. It also asked unrelated questions and is rejected. The candidate was unloaded, the existing 2B Q4 model was reloaded with HTTP 200 and verified in `ollama ps`, then the candidate was removed. No platform model selection was changed. These probes establish failed acceptance cases, not a statistically controlled speed ranking: cache/startup and request-guidance differences prevent that stronger conclusion. Official sources: https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B and https://ollama.com/library/qwen3.5:0.8b. LiquidAI's LFM2.5-1.2B card lists English, Arabic, Chinese, French, German, Japanese, Korean and Spanish; it does not establish Hindi support, so it was not downloaded as a Hindi replacement.
- `d2bdff33`: server output trace now separates the first transport packet from the first speech-energy frame and counts silence embedded between speech frames. Threshold is 80 RMS in 16-bit PCM, matching the in-place recording diagnosis. 17 focused observer checks passed, including leading silence, internal silence, and trailing silence behavior. Built and deployed only the API after zero active calls. This is diagnostic evidence for the next real call, not proof of caller playback latency or a fixed audio gap.
- `9019f444`: removed forced 48-character word-boundary splitting from follow-up TTS, preserving sentence streaming and initial natural-clause release. On the VPS, a synthetic three-request sentence produced internal silences of 0.48 and 0.58 seconds; the single-request comparison produced one 0.42-second pause, with 4.209-second synthesis versus 4.560 seconds. Both were faster than their 9.6 seconds of audio and neither showed simulated delivery underflow. The gateway may still bound long text internally; these measurements are not listening acceptance. Focused aggregator regression passed. API built and replaced after confirming zero active calls. Fresh caller-heard pause/latency verification remains open; longer sentence buffering can delay later phrases when Qwen is slow.
- `61fadd2f`: restores explicit English/Hindi reply choice from persisted user history when text chat recreates its model service, and makes contact-name acknowledgment honor the chosen English reply language. 72 focused checks passed, including the real text pipeline. API built and deployed on the VPS; running-container assertions passed. Full model-following and caller-heard acceptance remain open.
- `fe40962b`: production-source integration and conversation continuity changes deployed to API and UI. Both health checks returned healthy, zero restarts and HTTP 200. 109 focused backend checks and 9 conversation UI checks passed. Whole-platform acceptance remains open.
- `6339ab14`: corrected nested response boundaries in deterministic reply handlers. A real text-pipeline regression first reproduced the hang, then passed with the correction; 25 focused checks passed. API deployed and healthy. This supersedes the earlier constructor-only acknowledgment check as stronger evidence.
- Browser test run 407: the pre-fix request timed out and persisted a failed turn, advancing the session revision from 2 to 4. The browser's next retry returned HTTP 409 because it still held revision 2. No completed reply from this browser test is claimed.
- `3c0cbcef`: refresh failed chat session from the authenticated GET endpoint before a user retry. Preserves the typed draft and never resends automatically, preventing duplicate tool actions. Focused React hook test passed, including retry with the refreshed revision. UI image built on the VPS and deployed: `sha256:c91b6168717132e5e16210c29951a4e57d35191c080c579da892c7f604b7e1d6`. API and UI report healthy with zero restarts; public API health and UI login both return 200. The SSH observation dropped during the build; the original live process was polled until it exited and the new image appeared, then deployed without restarting the build.
- Browser automation became disabled in Work settings during the retry, so authenticated browser verification after these fixes is pending. No new telephone call has been made.
- Actual combined TTS inventory: Hindi `HIN_F` and Indian English `ENG_IN` both use Indus/Inaya. The endpoint advertises 15 languages and 22 voice IDs; voice count is not language coverage. All 22 scheduled languages, consistent male/female coverage, naturalness and code-switch quality are unaccepted.
- Numeric in-place recording inspection: run 405 contains within-response silence spans up to about 1.26 seconds; run 406 telephone first response still took 10.912 seconds. Transport silence frames invalidate the trace's first-frame value as proof of audible latency. The 700–800 ms end-to-end target is unmet.
- Tests, diagnostic scripts, reports and this tracker remain excluded from the new Git commits and GitHub. No model service or inference process was started on the local PC; temporary dependency junction used for the UI hook test was removed.

## Current local integration evidence — 29 October 2026

- Prompt Templates: added edit/delete controls for organization-owned custom templates, kept featured/system templates protected, and derive category filters from the templates returned by the API. The client uses the existing organization-scoped `PUT /prompt-templates/{id}` and `DELETE /prompt-templates/{id}` routes. Focused UI regression: **2/2 passed** on this worktree; `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check` passed. This is local source/test evidence only; no combined UI build, production deployment, or live template workflow is claimed.
- Cross-worktree coordination: sent current production speech/runtime status and UI acceptance status to both active Kodewaves chats, and identified the Prompt Templates files as uncommitted to prevent conflicting integration. The UI integration chat reports **81 focused licensing tests passed** with dummy database/Redis URLs; its real database migration, full-site UI build/tests, client-image signing/install/upgrade, and live deployment remain open. The audit chat is still active. No merge or deployment has occurred from this handoff.
- Telephony socket claim: code review found a read-then-write race where two callbacks could both observe an initialized workflow run and each transition it to running. Added a conditional database update scoped to run, workflow, and organization; only the successful first claim proceeds. The handler rejects an already-claimed run. An independent source review found the claim and tenant-scope predicates consistent with the existing flow. Python syntax compilation and whitespace validation passed. A temporary in-memory SQLite smoke exercised the current ORM method: first claims succeeded, replay and wrong workflow/org claims were rejected, and all rows ended in `running`. The dedicated PostgreSQL test was attempted but failed during fixture setup because its configured test endpoint refused the connection; the test body did not run. No production deployment has occurred; PostgreSQL concurrency and fresh telephony acceptance remain open. This local test/report is not intended for GitHub.
- Phone-number parser check against the caller's examples: the current parser already has cases for `nine five 383 double zero 941`, Hindi ASR spellings, split calls, and uncertain digits. Ran the focused pure parser/contact selection against the project test environment: **33 passed, 27 deselected**. That proves the exact supplied transcript text parses in isolation; it does not prove the live STT produced that exact text or that WebRTC used the deterministic handler. The next live-trace check must capture per-utterance STT finals and whether the phone handler or LLM answered before changing normalization rules.
- Tools UI: the `native` and built-in `integration` tool categories were labelled “Coming Soon” although the current workflow schema/runtime implements neither type. They now say “Not Available” and list working alternatives (End Call, Transfer Call, Transfer To Agent, Calculator, External HTTP API, and MCP Server). Focused UI tests passed **2/2**. The configured MPS service-key client exposes create/get/archive, but no provider reactivation operation; the existing reactivation **501** remains truthful. No deploy or client/provider capability change was made.
- Auto STT-to-local-TTS routing review: the suspected missing language-context link is already present in source. `run_pipeline.py` wires STT to TTS; `SpeachesLiveTTSService` resolves trusted `latest_tts_language` and sends `X-Kodewaves-Speech-Language`; the cached path includes the language in its cache key; and the local VITS gateway passes request/header language to its shared-script voice router. The focused existing language-context and auto-routing tests passed **52/52** on this worktree. This rejects “missing metadata wiring” as the demonstrated source-level cause; it does not verify accurate live detection, human-like audio, or reliable Hindi/English/Hinglish switching in calls.

## 25 October 2026 automatic spoken-language detector rejection

Official Vakgyata-tiny publishes Apache-2.0, 24M parameters and 12-language coverage, including Indian English, Hindi, Tamil, Telugu, Kannada and Malayalam. Its ONNX file was tested privately on the VPS in a disposable 2-core/1-GiB container. Six production-TTS-generated short utterances gave 71-139 ms inference after 16-kHz resampling: Hindi, Kannada and Malayalam were correct, while Indian English, Tamil and Telugu were wrong with near-1.0 reported top probabilities. This synthetic set is not a real-caller accuracy evaluation, but it falsifies the checkpoint as a safe automatic router for the requested main languages. No UI/backend auto selector was enabled, no runtime model changed, and the 93-MB temporary weights were removed. The all-22-language automatic detection requirement remains open. Model card: https://huggingface.co/onecxi/vakgyata-tiny .

## 24 October 2026: explicit recognition switch deployment verified
Production and local branch: `codex/production-integration`, source commit `521e2f62`. Running API image: `sha256:0df7fc5fd8790c0003878b6577b0c2d83871038798493c7b4c9bb7c222a5c60d`; container healthy, zero restarts. A probe inside the deployed API checked actual installed STT inventory and changed an isolated service instance Hindi -> English -> Hindi successfully. No organization setting or active call was changed by that probe. The 26 focused recognition/booking checks passed again after the inventory timeout was added. This proves installed-decoder switching only; automatic spoken-language identification, mixed-language transcription, caller-heard audio continuity and the end-to-end latency target remain unaccepted.

Correction to run 406 announcement timing: first above-threshold outgoing speech was approximately 646 ms after the media WebSocket connected, 201 ms after pipeline readiness, and 164 ms after TTS started. The earlier 165 ms figure used the TTS-start boundary rather than the WebSocket boundary. Carrier playback and answer-to-audio timing remain unverified.

### Conversation continuity checks, 24 October 2026
59 focused checks passed for text-session revisions/history, caller-language preference and caller contact context. Reviewed model-request construction: it copies message dictionaries, removes only the already-spoken opening greeting, and retains caller/assistant history; the 2B model still uses a 4096-token serving context. This does not prove the live model uses all older facts correctly or avoids server-side context eviction in a long call. Latest deployed API logs contain no new caller run after startup, so there is no fresh end-to-end acceptance evidence. Next investigation must distinguish model prompt/context capacity from transcript persistence using actual request token counts and a representative multi-turn replay; do not claim the reported forgetting fixed from these helper checks.

## Server-only model correctness comparison, 24 October 2026
Text encoding was verified through SSH: Devanagari code points arrived intact. A short supplied-name history sent to the selected Qwen3.5 2B Q4 returned a refusal to recall the supplied name (first text 1635 ms, wall 4048 ms, 116 input tokens). Explicit known-name guidance did not repair it. Removing sampling penalties also failed. Both chat rendering and manually rendered ChatML returned the same contradictory answer on an English-only name question, weakening a renderer-only hypothesis. The installed Q8 copy also failed that exact question (10016 ms wall including 6083 ms loading). A thinking-enabled Q4 probe spent 200 generated tokens / 13048 ms entirely on reasoning with no visible answer; it was not adopted.

Temporary official `qwen2.5:1.5b` candidate (Ollama Q4_K_M, 986MB) was tested only after active call count was zero. With a short receptionist system prompt: supplied-name memory returned correctly in 3524 ms first text including 2500 ms loading; Hindi 494 ms first text /1945 ms total but merely paraphrased the request; Hinglish 746/1445 ms asked again for the already supplied name and date; correction 555/959 ms used corrected name Ravi but asked again for the supplied date. Candidate rejected for context/slot correctness, unloaded and deleted. Selected `qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M` restored (HTTP200), `ollama ps` confirmed CPU residency and4096 context. No persisted model setting changed, no caller call was initiated, and no benchmark ran locally. These synthetic probes are not full live-flow acceptance or a guarantee of model-only speed under the real prompt.

Official candidate references: https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and https://ollama.com/library/qwen2.5:1.5b. Upstream cache issue reports concern other configurations and do not establish a cause on this VPS; no runtime upgrade was justified by those reports alone.

### Caller name memory fix deployed: 24 October 2026
Root cause confirmed: generic caller name statements were not saved in call state unless they answered the specific combined name-and-phone prompt. Qwen was also shown to contradict names present in history, so relying on the model to recall them was insufficient. Added shared call-state capture for explicit English/Hindi name statements, caller corrections, and compound requests; direct name-recall questions now use the stored value in both live calls and text chat. Model requests also receive a concise reminder when a name has been explicitly supplied. Focused contact/language checks: 50 passed. Server source branch advanced cleanly from 521e2f62 to ea273dff with only three tracked product files. API image sha256:915afb12a86fb33d3bade5c1bbc3f491a597cbd9dcf7f872b6936ba01f0181e4 is running healthy, zero restarts. Live container smoke captured आदर्श from a compound Hindi request and answered "What name did I give you?" with the stored name. Ollama remains on qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M at 100% CPU, 4096 context. No tests, diagnostics or reports were committed. This verifies the specific name recall path; broad context retention, other answered slots, TTS continuity and end-to-end latency remain unaccepted.

### Name-only caller answers: 24 October 2026
Extended name capture for the common case where the assistant asks “What is your name?” and the caller answers with only a short name. It requires a prior assistant name question, rejects digits and common yes/no responses, and keeps the result in the same call-scoped state used for direct recall, caller corrections and text chat. 52 focused contact/language checks passed. Source commit d209d5a2 is on the server branch; API image sha256:67d118e501e28e58277071cb6fb8c9e842f29a8aeddac12cdd2d61ea766f3d98 responds healthy with zero restarts. A live container smoke confirmed name-only Hindi capture and later direct recall. This does not resolve model forgetting of dates, booking intent, speech gaps or the latency objective.

### Regional TTS completion handoff: 24 October 2026
Root cause found in `api/local_models/indic_vits/app.py`: the TTS model worker thread directly called `asyncio.Queue.put_nowait(None)` to announce end-of-stream, while all audio chunks were enqueued via the event loop's thread-safe scheduler. The direct cross-thread queue mutation was unsafe and could leave the reader asleep after the final PCM chunk. Commit `610d35ec` fixes the completion callback to schedule the sentinel with `loop.call_soon_threadsafe`.

Before restarting the regional gateway, API workers on ports 8000 and 8001 both reported zero active calls. Only `kodewaves_indic_vits_tts` was rebuilt/restarted; API/UI/STT/Ollama/Indus and model volumes were left running. The new gateway image is `sha256:7657362410bfd34c6dcfb632d1fb3a6f8fedc19d8458c957a942fd77470ca266`, healthy with zero restarts. The in-container model-free worker-thread check delivered two chunks and closed the stream before its two-second deadline. A real short mixed Hindi/English synthesis returned first PCM at 150 ms and closed at 2,057 ms for 4,880 ms generated audio, with an 81 ms maximum read interval. API/UI stayed healthy; gateway memory was ~250 MiB after the request.

Acceptance limit: this verifies the TTS HTTP gateway's synthesis and stream close, not audible browser/WebRTC or telephone playout. No fresh real call has run after the fix, so mid-sentence interruption, phrase gaps and full-call latency remain open. Current model speech is still generated by Indus/Inaya for the supported Hindi/Indian-English voices; no replacement model was activated. The 35 platform acceptance findings above remain open pending a current production recheck.

### Telephony configuration retention: 24 October 2026
API startup contained an unconditional SQL delete for all tenant telephony configurations named `Dograh Cloudonix SIP` or `Kodewaves Cloudonix SIP`. These names are also used by the managed Cloudonix provisioner, so a normal API restart could erase a configured carrier, its attached numbers and trunks. Removed the startup delete in product commit `50643601` and fast-forwarded the server branch. Both API workers reported zero active calls before the API rebuild. The rebuilt API image `sha256:adc01701f6bde5225e7edbfe473059e8bca9e0e90e692795982d61e92ecccf17` reports healthy with zero restarts; the internal health route returns HTTP 200. The total telephony configuration count stayed at one before and after deployment. There was no Cloudonix row on this VPS at the time of deployment, so that count alone does not prove a Cloudonix row survives a subsequent restart. Authorized carrier call setup and voice quality also remain unverified.

### CPU LLM response gate: 24 October 2026
Server-only, no-active-call synthetic comparison of Workflow 21 draft 41 found that the resident selected Qwen3.5 2B Q4 spent 11.245 s evaluating a 1,053-token composed prompt before first visible text at 11.318 s; model load was 1 ms. A cached-prefix follow-up first text arrived in 1.004 s, but the answer still invented details. A candidate prompt of 522 tokens reduced uncached prefill to 5.469 s, yet hallucinated an unavailable transfer and failed an English-switch follow-up. The shorter candidate was rejected and no model or prompt setting changed. These probes are not a real WebRTC or telephony turn and leave the 700–800 ms response and conversation-quality acceptance gates open. Detailed numbers and limits are in `MASTER_VOICE_PIPELINE_PLAN.md`.

Its P3 claim that Ollama keep-alive/context were unset is contradicted by the live `kodewaves_ollama` container: `ollama ps` reports qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M resident on CPU with 4096 context and 12-month expiry; environment reports OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1 and OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=1. P4 remains a plausible contention mechanism but is unproven for recent calls: no context-summarization or variable-extraction events appeared in current 24-hour API logs, and summarization is skipped when <=6 context messages. These tasks preserve cross-node context and extracted variables, so they must not be removed without call-level evidence and replacement semantics. API restarted with zero active calls, reached healthy status and zero restarts. No end-to-end call evidence yet, so TTS continuity and first-audio latency remain unaccepted.

25 Oct 2026 speech diagnosis on the 4-vCPU VPS: With both API workers reporting zero active calls, three direct Hindi PCM requests through the production regional gateway/Inaya route measured (first PCM / full synthesis / generated audio): initial clause 9877/9917/2930 ms, next tail 474/1764/4170 ms, whole reply 589/4030/7780 ms. Repeating the same initial clause immediately gave 312/1563/2930 ms and 259/1478/2890 ms. The first high delay is a cold/contended observation, not attributed to model load without further traces; both TTS containers stayed healthy with zero restarts. A direct native Ollama request using selected qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M and a short Hindi receptionist prompt returned first text at 16627 ms, with prompt evaluation 13056 ms for 66 tokens and 54 output tokens in 2935 ms. Two immediate repeats returned first text at 168/154 ms; 80/63 output tokens took 4626/4051 ms, with ~1-2 ms model load and ~108-138 ms prompt evaluation. The first request followed a resource-heavy UI build, but causality is unproven. These measurements show a severe cold/contended outlier and that warm token generation still takes several seconds for a complete reply; the model's sample Hindi answer invented room details. Phrase splitting can therefore expose a pause while later LLM text arrives, but no real WebRTC/carrier audio trace yet proves that was the cause of the user's stop/start playback. Do not claim the 200 ms PCM buffer or current model meets the response target from these direct probes.

25 Oct 2026 host contention check: VPS has 4 logical CPUs, no swap, 5.7 GiB available RAM at observation; memory PSI 10-second average was 0, CPU PSI some 10-second average was 2.82. Host load average was 4.74 with 55 php-fpm processes from unrelated sites, while Kodewaves Ollama/TTS containers were near idle at the instant sampled. Model/TTS containers have CPU quotas of 3/1/2 cores respectively, so concurrent model work and other-site traffic can exceed four cores. This is evidence of potential CPU contention, not proof it caused the earlier cold request outliers; no unrelated process, site, cgroup or quota was changed. Before claiming a target latency, correlate per-call stage traces with simultaneous host load and compare quiet/busy intervals.

25 Oct 2026 live speech stage host-load instrumentation: commit 2434775f adds host 1-minute load average to existing STT-final, LLM-first-text and TTS-first-audio stage logs, keyed by run/workflow. It does not change inference or audio routing and adds no separate diagnostic process. Server branch fast-forwarded; the API source parsed successfully in the read-only container (py_compile could not write __pycache__ there), both workers had zero active calls, then only API restarted. API reached healthy with zero restarts and health HTTP 200; `os.getloadavg()` returned a numeric host value inside the API container. No real call has emitted the new three-stage log yet, so contention attribution remains unverified.

25 Oct 2026 explicit language-switch follow-up: The installed STT inventory advertises `hi-IN` and `en-IN` for IndicConformer. A fresh in-container call-scoped STT service successfully changed its selected decoder hi→en→hi via the live inventory; this did not change saved organization settings. The direct switch parser recognized two historically reported garbled utterances (`कन यूताार्क इन इंग्लिश`, `क्या तुम इंग्लिश में प कर सकते हो`) but missed `अंग्रेजी में बात करो`. Commit 4860745e adds common English imperatives (`बात करो/करें/करिए/कीजिए`) to the standalone switch route so both the reply preference and call-scoped recognizer update. Server source fast-forwarded; in-container parser checks passed for those variants and Hindi/English controls. Both workers were at zero active calls before only API restarted; it is healthy with zero restarts. This is not automatic language identification and is not a caller-heard voice acceptance test.

25 Oct 2026 live transcript key fix: `useWebSocketRTC.tsx` retained finalized message objects, but generated all React message keys with `Date.now()`; two events of the same kind within one millisecond could collide. Commit 4eb38285 adds a monotonic sequence suffix for all generated live feedback IDs. This removes one concrete rendering-identity risk without changing message content, state retention, or transcript persistence. Production UI image `sha256:2a8a3dd236980ae539099ed7761a343c2b2e81a5865a858ac90ef4374c3310a0` built successfully; Next build still skips separate type/lint checks. The earlier report of old bubbles disappearing is not accepted as fixed until a live visual session confirms it; LLM memory is a separate path.

25 Oct 2026 contact-state correction: `remember_explicit_caller_name` could treat a brief spoken number word as a valid name if a previous assistant turn asked for name and phone, overwriting a correct caller name. Commit 0a05b490 rejects recognized digit words in `_looks_like_name`. In a fresh production API-container probe, dialogue `मेरा नाम आदर्श है` followed by `फोर है` retained `caller_name=आदर्श`, collected `caller_phone_digits=4`, and asked for the remaining digits. Both API workers had zero active calls before only API restarted; it is healthy with zero restarts and HTTP 200. This proves that specific state path, not complete phone-number recognition or conversation memory across every node/call.

25 Oct 2026 assessment of pasted four-stage scan: Several cited paths are real but its proposed fixes cannot all be applied as written. The current code already uses a 200 ms TTS PCM chunk, a 12-character first completed sentence threshold, and parallel independent WebRTC preflight; production Ollama has Qwen3.5 2B Q4 resident at context 4096 with keep-alive forever and one parallel slot. The warmup sidecar sends generation only when the configured model is absent from `/api/ps`; it is not generating every 30 seconds. The pre-call ringer's 10-second gate is conditional on an enabled pre-call URL, not unconditional. A date-sensitive system prompt does change with the minute; freezing its clock for an entire call would risk incorrect booking times, so no such change was deployed. Booking-tool suppression is a consent safeguard and cannot simply be removed for cache stability. The reported 109–117 ms / 19.65 s prompt comparison is not an isolated controlled proof that prefix invalidation alone caused the delay; the direct VPS probes showed cold model requests and CPU contention can also contribute. Next meaningful evidence is a real run's STT-final, LLM-first-text and TTS-first-audio trace together with whether pre-call fetch ran, actual prompt tokens and host load. No new caller run was in the available API logs at this check. The pasted 700–800 ms projections remain unverified.

25 Oct 2026 follow-up memory/CPU correction: Workflow 21 production configuration has `context_compaction_enabled=true`. The Pipecat summarization utility summarizes every eligible message before the last N, so the previous N=2 could discard booking details after only a few exchanges; each summary also invokes the same local LLM and competes with the caller's next response. Commit `b64fe196` raises retention to eight messages (typically four caller/assistant exchanges) and requires at least four older messages before starting summary inference. Pushed to `origin/codex/production-integration`, fast-forwarded the server branch from `0a05b490` to `b64fe196`, and restarted only API after both workers reported zero active calls. API source parses in-container, the runtime manager reports retention=8, API health is HTTP 200 and container health is healthy with zero restarts. No live multi-turn call has yet proven caller-detail retention or reduced latency; the change is deployed but needs caller acceptance evidence.

25 Oct 2026 production text-chat failure analysis: Workflow run 407 is a real text-chat session. Its first turn completed with a 103-character assistant reply; the next turn failed and stored no assistant reply. The saved error was `Timed out waiting for text chat response window to settle (pending_context_requests=0, active_llm_completions=0, active_assistant_segments=1, blocking_tool_calls=[])`. This established that the timeout was gated by a stale assistant-segment counter after LLM completion. Commit `241ef53f` makes completed LLM generations the text-chat settle boundary and stores streamed model text as a fallback if the aggregator stop callback is missing. Pushed and fast-forwarded production from `b64fe196` to `241ef53f`; both call workers showed zero active calls before only API restart. The API source parsed in-container, and API health returned HTTP 200 with healthy status and zero restarts. This is deployed code evidence; no new user text-chat turn was generated, so recovery behavior still needs a real turn acceptance check.

## 25 October 2026 WebRTC history and live-chat verification status

The deployed live-transcript UI maps the full `feedbackMessages` array into the timeline; there is no history truncation (`slice`/message limit) in the WebRTC hook. The timeline auto-follows the latest item when the user is at the bottom, which moves older bubbles above the viewport; scrolling upward pauses follow mode. Thus disappearing-from-view in the screenshot is explained by scrolling, not evidence that the UI deleted old bubbles. Whether the model receives and uses prior turns is still a separate open question: the latest run 408 trace contains one caller turn (`history_messages=3`, `history_chars=84`) and cannot verify multi-turn memory. A post-deploy multi-turn trace with two caller turns and supplied contact details is required.

Production `codex/production-integration` is at `64d1082b7`; the API is healthy and the configured Ollama request builder supplies 3 threads. The deployment has not yet been exercised in a fresh live WebRTC call. Browser automation reports `Local browser use is disabled in Work settings`, and the Chrome window exposed to Windows automation is a separate YouTube page; neither was touched. This prevents agent-driven browser acceptance in the present environment, but does not prevent further code review. The text-chat retry/revision fix is deployed but its live UI retry journey remains open.

### 25 October 2026 live Workflow 22 delay and phone-number follow-up

A real production call (run 409) confirms the reported delay. STT final was logged at 08:20:54.196 UTC; the first LLM text was logged at 08:21:14.890 UTC, and the UI's first-token measurement was 20,339 ms. Ollama handled the first request in 22.156 s: 1,086 prompt tokens had no cached prefix, prompt evaluation took 13.586 s (79.93 tokens/s), and generating 22 tokens took 1.896 s. Its llama runner reported loading at 08:21:01, about 6.8 s after the STT final. The following requests reused 1,082/1,148 and 1,144/1,310 prompt tokens; first output fell to 1.114 s and 2.288 s. In the same call, TTS produced first PCM in 244 ms after the TTS request. This directly locates the 20 s spike in the cold LLM runner/prompt-prefill path; it does not show that the first-turn delay has been fixed.

Phone-number follow-up root cause was reproduced in the handler: Hindi ASR spellings such as `थी`, `आईट`, `डबल`, and `नान` were not consistently converted to digits, and an unclear full retry could append to the previous partial attempt. Commit `167f4aaf` adds those ASR variants, expands `double zero`, asks for the full number again after an unclear digit, and replaces the previous attempt on that retry. Focused tests: `api/tests/test_contact_context.py` — 17 passed. The source-only commit is on `codex/production-integration`; no tests or reports were pushed. After confirming zero active calls, the production API was restarted. API health is 200/healthy, active calls 0, event-loop lag p95 1.9 ms, and an in-container Hindi digit/double-zero plus retry replacement smoke passed.

Acceptance limits: no new user-started call has yet validated the phone correction end to end. The observed 20,339 ms first-token delay remains open. Warm turns were faster due to prompt-cache reuse, but that does not satisfy fresh-call latency; investigate why the cold runner load and full 1,086-token prefill happen on the first turn, then verify in a fresh call. Do not treat API health or the deterministic phone smoke as full WebRTC/telephony acceptance.

### 25 October 2026 VPS Qwen3.5 0.8B candidate comparison

With production reporting zero active calls, ran the same four synthetic Hindi, Hinglish, Indian-English and digit-recall prompts through the current `qwen3.5:2b-q4_K_M` and candidate `qwen3.5:0.8b` on the VPS (CPU, 3 threads, context 4096, thinking disabled). These prompts were ~2,170 tokens, about twice the 1,086-token first prompt in run 409; treat timings as a controlled relative probe, not a production-latency estimate.

Current 2B first visible text: 27.8 s on its cold request, then 5.5–7.9 s across the three warm prompts. The 0.8B candidate: 23.3 s cold, then 4.2 s (English prompt), 4.5 s (digit recall), and 32.0 s (Hinglish prompt). The candidate did preserve the synthetic phone digits but answered the English prompt in Hindi and gave a mismatched response to the Hindi booking request; results did not meet language-following/grounding needs and latency was not reliably lower. Rejected and removed from the Ollama store. Production routing/config remains on 2B Q4; it was warmed back after the probe and `ollama ps` confirmed it resident. API active calls remained zero at restoration. No candidate or report was pushed.

This narrows the next latency work: do not swap to 0.8B; address why run 409 paid a 6.3 s cold runner load plus 13.6 s uncached 1,086-token prompt prefill, then validate a fresh call. Candidate scores are synthetic and not an end-to-end live-call acceptance.

25 Oct 2026 warmup follow-up: Checked the suspected context mismatch on the VPS with no active calls. The sidecar-style empty prompt and a production-shaped request both report `context_length=4096`; that hypothesis is rejected. A deliberately cold reload took 4.85 s, matching the order of the runner-load component in run 409, while the subsequent production-shaped request used the loaded runner.
